<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=center><B>PART I</B></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt"><U>ITEM 1. &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;BUSINESS</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt"><U>General Development of Business</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><A NAME="OLE_LINK1"></A><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt">In this 10-K, &#147;we&#148;, &#147;us&#146;, &#147;our&#148;, or &#147;the Company&#148;, refers to Monmouth Capital Corporation, together with its predecessors and subsidiaries, unless the context requires otherwise.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt">Monmouth Capital Corporation is a corporation organized in the State of New Jersey in 1961. &nbsp;The Company operates as a qualified real estate investment trust (REIT) under Sections 856-860 of the Internal Revenue Code (the Code) and intends to maintain its qualification as a REIT in the future. &nbsp;As a qualified REIT, with limited exceptions, the Company will not be taxed under federal and certain state income tax laws at the corporate level on taxable income that it distributes to its shareholders. &nbsp;For special tax provisions applicable to REITS, refer to Sections 856-860 of the Code. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt">Prior to 1994 the Company operated as a small business investment company. &nbsp;During 1994, the Company formed a wholly-owned subsidiary, The Mobile Home Store, Inc. (MHS), to finance and sell manufactured homes. &nbsp;The sales operation was conducted at manufactured home communities owned by United Mobile Homes (United), a related REIT. &nbsp;&nbsp;The Company also invested in real estate and securities. &nbsp;In March 2001, the Company sold the existing inventory of manufactured homes to United at the Company&#146;s carrying value and the Company exited the manufactured home sales business since it proved to be unprofitable. &nbsp;MHS was merged into the Company on December 6, 2001.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt">On September 26, 2001, the Company adopted a change from a fiscal year end of March 31 to a calendar year end, effective for the short year ended December 31, 2001. &nbsp;The Company elected to be taxed as a real estate investment trust for the transition period ended December 31, 2001. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>

<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-3-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company is currently operating as a diversified REIT investing in real estate equities, mortgages, mortgage-backed securities, and other REIT securities. &nbsp;The Company&#146;s capital is limited and there is no assurance the Company can or will continue to operate as a diversified REIT. &nbsp;The Company will consider alternative plans or proposals.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Narrative Description of the Business</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Currently the Company derives its income primarily through real estate rental operations and from dividend and interest income. &nbsp;The Company also derives some revenues from the sales of manufactured homes which the Company has repossessed and returned to inventory. &nbsp;These manufactured homes were originally sold prior to March 30, 2001, when the Company was in the manufactured home sales business. &nbsp;Rental income and reimbursements were $4,881,491, $2,835,614, $1,734,583 for the years ended December 31, 2005, 2004 and 2003, respectively. &nbsp;Interest and dividend income was $1,135,290, $1,247,988 and $1,091,924, for the years ended December 31, 2005, 2004 and 2003 respectively. &nbsp;Sales of manufactured homes were $79,305, $165,324 and $269,690 for the years ended December 31, 2005, 2004 and 2003 respectively. &nbsp;&nbsp;Total assets were $69,254,054, $54,149,086, and $41,569,008 at December 31, 2005, 2004, and 2003, respectively.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company has approximately 779,000 square feet of property, of which approximately 383,000 square feet, or 49% is leased to Federal Express Corporation and subsidiaries. &nbsp;During 2005, 2004 and 2003, rental income and reimbursements from properties leased to Federal Express Corporation and subsidiaries approximated 47%, 77% and 75%, respectively of total rental income and reimbursements.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>At December 31, 2005, the Company had investments in eleven industrial warehouse properties (see item 2 for detailed description of properties). &nbsp;The Company seeks to invest in additional properties and anticipates acquisitions of approximately $20,000,000 in 2006. &nbsp;&nbsp;The funds for these acquisitions may come from the Company&#146;s bank borrowings, the Dividend Reinvestment and Stock Purchase Plan (the DRIP), the Convertible Subordinated Debentures (the Debentures), and other private placements. &nbsp;To the extent that funds or appropriate properties are not available, fewer acquisitions will be made. &nbsp;Because of the contingent nature of contracts to purchase real property, the Company announces acquisitions only on closing. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company raises capital through the DRIP in which participants purchase stock from the Company at a discount of approximately 5% of market price. &nbsp;During 2005, approximately $5,000,000 additional capital was raised through the DRIP. &nbsp;It is anticipated, although no assurances can be given, that a comparable level of participation will continue in the DRIP in 2006. &nbsp;In addition, on March 31, 2005, the Company completed a private placement offering of $10,420,000 of 8% Convertible Subordinated Debentures, due 2015. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company competes with other investors in real estate for attractive investment opportunities. &nbsp;These investors include other &#147;equity&#148; real estate investment trusts, limited partnerships, syndications and private investors, among others. &nbsp;Competition in the market areas in which the Company operates is significant and affects acquisitions and/or development of properties, occupancy levels, rental rates and operating expenses of certain properties. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-4-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Management has built relationships with merchant builders which provide the Company with investment opportunities which fit the Company&#146;s investment policy. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company operates as part of a group of three public companies (all REITs) which includes United Mobile Homes, Inc. (United) and Monmouth Real Estate Investment Corporation (MREIC). &nbsp;United specializes in investments in manufactured home communities and MREIC specializes in net-leased industrial properties to investment grade tenants on long term leases. &nbsp;It is intended that the Company will invest in real estate ventures that do not qualify under the investment objectives of United and MREIC. &nbsp;To the extent there may be conflicts of interest as to prospective investments, the Company may be deprived of investment opportunities. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company has six full-time employees who also provide services to United and MREIC. &nbsp;&nbsp;Additional salaries are allocated as professional fees from United based on the time employees devote to the Company. &nbsp;Some general and administrative expenses are allocated to the Company from MREIC and United based on use or services provided. &nbsp;&nbsp;A Board of Directors consisting of seven members is responsible for the general policies of the Company.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company does not have an advisory contract; however, all of the properties are managed by various management companies. &nbsp;Management fees were $23,266, $21,341, and $15,397 for 2005, 2004 and 2003, respectively. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company is subject to various environmental and regulatory requirements related to the ownership of real estate. &nbsp;Investments in real property and have the potential for environmental liability on the part of the owner of such property. &nbsp;The Company is not aware of any environmental liabilities to the Company relating to the Company&#146;s investment properties which would have a material adverse effect on the Company&#146;s business, assets or results of operations.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company continues to invest in both debt and equity securities of other REITs and mortgage backed securities. &nbsp;The Company from time to time may purchase these securities on margin when the interest and dividend yields exceed the cost of funds. &nbsp;The securities portfolio provides the Company with liquidity and additional income. &nbsp;&nbsp;Such securities are subject to risk arising from adverse changes in market rates and prices, primarily interest rate risk relating to debt securities and equity price risk relating to equity securities. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Additional information about the Company can be found on the Company&#146;s website which is located at <FONT COLOR=#0000FF><U>www.monmouthcapital.com</U></FONT>. &nbsp;The Company&#146;s filings with the Securities and Exchange Commission are made available through a link on the Company&#146;s website or by calling Investor Relations at 732-577-9993. &nbsp;&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>

<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-5-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>ITEM 1A. &nbsp;&#150; RISK FACTORS</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">Real Estate Industry Risks</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:4.5pt; text-indent:31.5pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company faces risks associated with local real estate conditions in areas where the Company owns properties. The Company may be affected adversely by general economic conditions and local real estate conditions. For example, an oversupply of industrial properties in a local area or a decline in the attractiveness of our properties to tenants would have a negative effect on the Company. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:40.5pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Other factors that may affect general economic conditions or local real estate conditions include:</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-14pt; padding-left:66pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-family:Symbol; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Symbol">&#183;</FONT></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:66pt; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">population and demographic trends;</FONT></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-14pt; padding-left:66pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-family:Symbol; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Symbol">&#183;</FONT></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:66pt; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">zoning, use and other regulatory restrictions;</FONT></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-14pt; padding-left:66pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-family:Symbol; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Symbol">&#183;</FONT></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:66pt; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">income tax laws;</FONT></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-14pt; padding-left:66pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-family:Symbol; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Symbol">&#183;</FONT></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:66pt; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">changes in interest rates and availability and costs of financing;</FONT></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-14pt; padding-left:66pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-family:Symbol; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Symbol">&#183;</FONT></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:66pt; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">competition from other available real estate;</FONT></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-14pt; padding-left:66pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-family:Symbol; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Symbol">&#183;</FONT></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:66pt; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">our ability to provide adequate maintenance and insurance; and</FONT></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-14pt; padding-left:66pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-family:Symbol; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Symbol">&#183;</FONT></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:66pt; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">increased operating costs, including insurance premiums and real estate taxes.</FONT></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company may be unable to compete with its larger competitors and other alternatives available to tenants or potential tenants of our properties. The real estate business is highly competitive. The Company competes for properties with other real estate investors, including other real estate investment trusts, limited partnerships, syndications and private investors, many of whom have greater financial resources, revenues, and geographical diversity than the Company. Furthermore, the Company competes for tenants with other property owners. All of the Company&#146;s industrial properties are subject to significant local competition. The Company also competes with a wide variety of institutions and other investors for capital funds necessary to support our investment activities and asset growth.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company is subject to significant regulation that inhibits our activities and increases our costs. Local zoning and use laws, environmental statutes and other governmental requirements may restrict expansion, rehabilitation and reconstruction activities. These regulations may prevent the Company from taking advantage of economic opportunities. Legislation such as the Americans with Disabilities Act may require management to modify our properties. Future legislation may impose additional requirements. The Company cannot predict what requirements may be enacted or what changes may be implemented to existing legislation.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-6-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">Risks Associated with Our Properties</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company may be unable to renew leases or relet space as leases expire. While management seeks to invest in well-located, modern buildings leased to credit-worthy tenants on long term leases, a number of the Company&#146;s properties are subject to short-term leases. When a lease expires, a tenant may elect not to renew it. Management may not be able to relet the property on similar terms, if we are able to relet the property at all. Management has established an annual budget for renovation and reletting expenses that management believes is reasonable in light of each property's operating history and local market characteristics. This budget, however, may not be sufficient to cover these expenses.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company has been and may continue to be affected negatively by tenant financial difficulties and leasing delays. A general decline in the economy may result in a decline in the demand for industrial space. As a result, the Company&#146;s tenants may delay lease commencement, fail to make rental payments when due, or declare bankruptcy. Any such event could result in the termination of that tenant's lease and losses to the Company. The Company receives a substantial portion of our income as rents under long-term leases. If tenants are unable to comply with the terms of their leases because of rising costs or falling sales, management, in our sole discretion, may deem it advisable to modify lease terms to allow tenants to pay a lower rental or a smaller share of operating costs, taxes and insurance.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company may be unable to sell properties when appropriate because real estate investments are illiquid. Real estate investments generally cannot be sold quickly and, therefore, will tend to limit management&#146;s ability to vary our property portfolio promptly in response to changes in economic or other conditions. The inability to respond promptly to changes in the performance of the Company&#146;s property portfolio could adversely affect the Company&#146;s financial condition and ability to service debt and make distributions to our stockholders.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:45.8pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Environmental liabilities could affect the Company&#146;s profitability. The Company faces possible environmental liabilities. Current and former real estate owners and operators may be required by law to investigate and clean up hazardous substances released at the properties they own or operate. They may also be liable to the government or to third parties for property damage, investigation costs and cleanup costs. Contamination may adversely affect the owner's ability to sell or lease real estate or to borrow using the real estate as collateral.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Environmental laws today can impose liability on a previous owner or operator of a property that owned or operated the property at a time when hazardous or toxic substances were disposed on, or released from, the property. A conveyance of the property, therefore, does not relieve the owner or operator from liability.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Management is not aware of any environmental liabilities relating to our investment properties which would have a material adverse effect on our business, assets, or results of operations. However, we cannot assure you that environmental liability claims will not arise in the future.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-7-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>If our insurance coverage is inadequate or management cannot obtain acceptable insurance coverage, the Company operations could be materially adversely affected. Management generally maintains insurance policies related to the Company&#146;s business, including casualty, general liability and other policies covering business operations, employees and assets. The Company may be required to bear all losses that are not adequately covered by insurance. Although management believes that our insurance programs are adequate, no assurance can be given that we will not incur losses in excess of the Company&#146;s insurance coverage, or that the Company will be able to obtain insurance in the future at acceptable levels and reasonable cost.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">Financing Risks</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company faces risks generally associated with our debt. The Company finances a portion of our investments in properties and marketable securities through debt. This debt creates risks, including:</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-14pt; padding-left:66pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-family:Symbol; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Symbol">&#183;</FONT></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:66pt; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">rising interest rates on our floating rate debt;</FONT></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-14pt; padding-left:66pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-family:Symbol; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Symbol">&#183;</FONT></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:66pt; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">failure to repay or refinance existing debt as it matures, which may result in the forced &nbsp;disposition of assets on disadvantageous terms;</FONT></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-14pt; padding-left:66pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-family:Symbol; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Symbol">&#183;</FONT></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:66pt; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">refinancing terms less favorable than the terms of existing debt; and</FONT></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-14pt; padding-left:66pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-family:Symbol; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Symbol">&#183;</FONT></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:66pt; font-size:12pt"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman">failure to meet required payments of principal and/or interest.</FONT></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company faces risks associated with the use of debt to fund acquisitions, including refinancing risk. The Company is subject to the risks normally associated with debt financing, including the risk that our cash flow will be insufficient to meet required payments of principal and interest. Management anticipates that a portion of the principal of our debt will not be repaid prior to maturity. Therefore, the Company will likely need to refinance at least a portion of our outstanding debt as it matures. There is a risk that we may not be able to refinance existing debt or that the terms of any refinancing will not be as favorable as the terms of the existing debt. If principal payments due at maturity cannot be refinanced, extended or repaid with proceeds from other sources, such as new equity capital or sales of properties, the Company&#146;s cash flow will not be sufficient to repay all maturing debt in years when significant &quot;balloon&quot; payments come due. As a result, we may be forced to dispose of properties on disadvantageous terms.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Other Risks</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Management may amend our business policies without the stockholders&#146; approval. Our board of directors determines our growth, investment, financing, capitalization, borrowing, REIT status, operations and distributions policies. Although the board of directors has no present intention to amend or reverse any of these policies, they may be amended or revised without notice to stockholders. Accordingly, stockholders may not have control over changes in our policies. Management cannot assure you that changes in our policies will serve fully the interests of all stockholders.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-8-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:45.8pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The market value of our Common Stock could decrease based on the Company&#146;s performance and market perception and conditions. The market value of the Company&#146;s Common Stock may be based primarily upon the market's perception of the Company&#146;s growth potential and current and future cash dividends, and may be secondarily based upon the real estate market value of the Company&#146;s underlying assets. The market price of the Company&#146;s Common Stock is influenced by the dividend on the Company&#146;s Common Stock relative to market interest rates. Rising interest rates may lead potential buyers of the Company&#146;s Common Stock to expect a higher dividend rate, which would adversely affect the market price of our Common Stock. In addition, rising interest rates would result in increased expense, thereby adversely affecting cash flow and the Company&#146;s ability to service our indebtedness and pay dividends.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:45.8pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>There are restrictions on the transfer of the Company&#146;s Common Stock. To maintain the Company&#146;s qualification as a REIT under the Internal Revenue Code of 1986 (the Code), no more than 50% in value of the Company&#146;s outstanding capital stock may be owned, actually or by attribution, by five or fewer individuals, as defined in the Code to also include certain entities, during the last half of a taxable year. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:45.8pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company&#146;s earnings are dependent, in part, upon the performance of our investment portfolio. As permitted by the Code, management invests in and owns securities of other real estate investment trusts. To the extent that the value of those investments declines or those investments do not provide a return, the Company&#146;s earnings could be adversely affected.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:45.8pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company may fail to qualify as a REIT. If the Company fails to qualify as a REIT, the Company will not be allowed to deduct distributions to stockholders in computing our taxable income and will be subject to Federal and state income tax, including any applicable alternative minimum tax, at regular corporate rates. In addition, the Company might be barred from qualification as a REIT for the four years following disqualification. The additional tax incurred at regular corporate rates would reduce significantly the cash flow available for distribution to stockholders and for debt service. &nbsp;Furthermore, the Company would no longer be required to make any distributions to the Company&#146;s stockholders as a condition to REIT qualification. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:45.8pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>To qualify as a REIT, and to continue to qualify as a REIT, the Company must comply with certain highly technical and complex requirements. The Company cannot be certain it has complied, and will always be able to comply, with these requirements. In addition, facts and circumstances that may be beyond the Company&#146;s control may affect the Company&#146;s ability to continue to qualify as a REIT. The Company cannot assure you that new legislation, regulations, administrative interpretations or court decisions will not change the tax laws significantly with respect to the Company&#146;s qualification as a REIT or with respect to the Federal income tax consequences of qualification. The Company believes that it has qualified as a REIT since its inception and intends to continue to qualify as a REIT. However, the Company cannot assure you that the Company is qualified or will remain qualified.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-9-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:45.8pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company may be unable to comply with the strict income distribution requirements applicable to REITs. To obtain the favorable tax treatment associated with qualifying as a REIT, among other requirements, the Company is required each year to distribute to its stockholders at least 90% of its REIT taxable income. The Company will be subject to corporate income tax on any undistributed REIT taxable income. In addition, we will incur a 4% nondeductible excise tax on the amount by which our distributions in any calendar year are less than the sum of (i) 85% of our ordinary income for the year, (ii) 95% of our capital gain net income for the year, and (iii) any undistributed taxable income from prior years. The Company could be required to borrow funds on a short-term basis to meet the distribution requirements that are necessary to achieve the tax benefits associated with qualifying as a REIT (and to avoid corporate income tax and the 4% excise tax), even if conditions were not favorable for borrowing.</P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:45.8pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Notwithstanding the Company&#146;s status as a REIT, the Company is subject to various Federal, state and local taxes on our income and property. For example, the Company will be taxed at regular corporate rates on any undistributed taxable income, including undistributed net capital gains, provided, however, that properly designated undistributed capital gains will effectively avoid taxation at the stockholder level. The Company may also have to pay some state income or franchise taxes because not all states treat REITs in the same manner as they are treated for Federal income tax purposes.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>ITEM 1B. &nbsp;&#150; UNRESOLVED STAFF COMMENTS</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>None.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-10-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt"><U>ITEM 2. &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;PROPERTIES</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company operates as a real estate investment trust. &nbsp;Its portfolio is primarily in real estate holdings, carried on the financial statements of the Company at depreciated cost. &nbsp;It is believed that their current market values exceed both the original cost and the depreciated cost. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>

<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-15-</P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">*</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">*</P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:45.8pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>* Funds from operations (FFO), is defined as net income, excluding gains (or losses) from sales of depreciable assets, plus depreciation. &nbsp;&nbsp;FFO should be considered as a supplemental measure of operating performance used by real estate investment trusts (REITs). &nbsp;FFO excludes &nbsp;historical cost &nbsp;depreciation &nbsp;as &nbsp;an expense and &nbsp;may &nbsp;facilitate &nbsp;the comparison &nbsp;of REITs which have different cost &nbsp;basis. &nbsp;&nbsp;The items excluded from FFO are significant components in understanding the Company's financial performance. FFO (1) does not represent cash flow from operations as defined &nbsp;by &nbsp;generally accepted accounting &nbsp;principles; &nbsp;(2) should not be considered as an alternative to net income &nbsp;as a &nbsp;measure &nbsp;of operating performance or to cash &nbsp;flows &nbsp;from operating, &nbsp;investing and financing activities; and &nbsp;(3) &nbsp;is not &nbsp;an &nbsp;alternative to cash flow as a measure of liquidity. &nbsp;&nbsp;FFO, as calculated by the Company, may not be comparable to similarly entitled measures reported by other REITs.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company&#146;s FFO is calculated as follows:</P>

<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt"><U>ITEM 7. &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;MANAGEMENT'S DISCUSSION AND ANALYSIS OF FINANCIAL</U></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<U>CONDITION AND RESULTS OF OPERATION</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt"><B><U>Overview</U></B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:45.8pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The following discussion and analysis of the consolidated financial condition and results of operations should be read in conjunction with the Consolidated Financial Statements and notes thereto elsewhere herein.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:45.8pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company is a diversified REIT. &nbsp;Currently, the Company&#146;s primary business is the ownership of eleven industrial properties subject to medium term leases and investing in marketable securities. These securities include securities of other REITS and mortgage backed securities. The Company also has loans receivable and inventory related to the sales of manufactured homes. &nbsp;Prior to March 31, 2001, the Company was engaged in the manufactured home sales and finance business. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:45.8pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company&#146;s revenue primarily consists of rental and related income from the ownership of the industrial properties, interest and dividend income, and gains on securities transactions. &nbsp;&nbsp;Sales of manufactured homes relates to the sale of inventory which had been repossessed and resold.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Although the Company currently owns eleven industrial properties, management would consider other types of real estate acquisitions. Management anticipates that the Company will acquire approximately $20,000,000 in properties during 2006. &nbsp;The current acquisitions environment is competitive and management may not be able to locate suitable properties for acquisition. &nbsp;&nbsp;Rental income and reimbursements increased $2,045,877 or 72% for the year ended December 31, 2005 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2004. &nbsp;&nbsp;This increase is due mainly to the rent and reimbursements related to the new property acquisitions in 2004 and 2005. &nbsp;Net income decreased $70,100 or 6% for the year ended December 31, 2005 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2004. &nbsp;This decrease in net income is mainly due to a decrease in gain on securities transactions, net, a decrease in interest and dividend income and increases in real estate taxes, interest expense and depreciation. &nbsp;The decrease in net income was partially offset by increases in rental income and reimbursements.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:45.8pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company has financed acquisitions through capital raised through the Company&#146;s Dividend Reinvestment and Stock Purchase Plan, by obtaining mortgages, and from private placement offerings, including the Convertible Subordinated Debentures. &nbsp;If suitable acquisitions cannot be found during 2006, the Company may invest additional capital raised in REIT securities or pay down outstanding debt. &nbsp;The Company also invests in debt and equity securities of other REITs for liquidity and additional income. &nbsp;The Company from time to time may purchase these securities on margin when there is an adequate yield spread. &nbsp;&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:45.8pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>See PART I, Item 1. Business for a more complete discussion of the economic and industry-wide factors relevant to the Company, the Company's lines of business and principal products and services, and the opportunities, challenges and risks on which the Company is focused.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-17-</P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">*</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt"><B><U>Significant Accounting Policies &amp; Estimates</U></B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The discussion and analysis of the Company&#146;s financial condition and results of operations are based upon the Company&#146;s consolidated financial statements, which have been prepared &nbsp;&nbsp;in &nbsp;&nbsp;accordance &nbsp;&nbsp;with &nbsp;&nbsp;accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The preparation of these financial statements requires management to make estimates and judgments that affect the reported amounts of assets and liabilities, revenues and expenses, and related disclosure of contingent assets and liabilities at the date of the Company&#146;s financial statements. Actual results may differ from these estimates under different assumptions or conditions. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;</P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Significant accounting policies are defined as those that involve significant judgment and potentially could result in materially different results under different assumptions and conditions. Management believes the following significant accounting policies are affected by our more significant judgments and estimates used in the preparation of the Company&#146;s financial statements. For a detailed description of these and other accounting policies, see Note 1 in the notes to the Company&#146;s financial statements included in this Form 10-K. &nbsp;Management has discussed each of these significant accounting policies with the audit committee of the Board of Directors.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<I>Real Estate Investments</I></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company applies Financial Accounting Standards Board Statement No. 144, &#147;Accounting for the Impairment or Disposal of Long-Lived Assets&#148;, to measure impairment in real estate investments. Rental properties are individually evaluated for impairment when conditions exist which may indicate that it is probable that the sum of expected future cash flows (on an undiscounted basis without interest) from a rental property is less than its historical net cost basis. These expected future cash flows consider factors such as future operating income, trends and prospects as well as the effects of leasing demand, competition and other factors. Upon determination that a permanent impairment has occurred, rental properties are reduced to their fair value. For properties to be disposed of, an impairment loss is recognized when the fair value of the property, less the estimated cost to sell, is less than the carrying amount of the property measured at the time there is a commitment to sell the property and/or it is actively being marketed for sale. A property to be disposed of is reported at the lower of its carrying amount or its estimated fair value, less its cost to sell. Subsequent to the date that a property is held for disposition, depreciation expense is not recorded. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<I>Securities Available for Sale</I></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Investments in liquid real estate assets consist primarily of marketable equity REIT securities. &nbsp;Management reviews our marketable securities for impairment on an annual basis, or when events or circumstances occur. If a decline in fair value is determined to be other than temporary, an impairment charge is recognized in earnings and the cost basis of the individual</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-18-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>security shall be written down to fair value as the &nbsp;new cost basis. &nbsp;Management&#146;s evaluation includes consideration of events that may be attributable to the unrealized loss, including among other things, the credit-worthiness of the issuer, length of time that a security had a continuous unrealized loss, and the financial position of the issuing company.</P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;</P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>&nbsp;<I>Estimates and Revenue Recognition </I></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Estimates are used to establish amounts receivable from tenants for such things as annualized rents, real estate taxes and other cost recoveries. In addition, an estimate is made with respect to whether a provision for allowance for doubtful accounts receivable and loans<BR>
receivable is necessary. The allowance for doubtful accounts reflects management&#146;s estimate of the amounts of the recorded accounts receivable and loans receivable at the balance sheet date that will not be realized from cash receipts in subsequent periods. If cash receipts in subsequent periods vary from our estimates, or if the Company&#146;s tenants&#146; financial condition deteriorates as a result of operating difficulties, additional changes to the allowance may be required.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><B><U>Results of Operation</U></B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Year Ended December 31, 2005 vs. Year Ended December 31, 2004</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Income is comprised primarily of rental income and reimbursements, interest and dividend income and gain on securities transactions. &nbsp;On March 30, 2001, the Company exited the manufactured home sales business since it proved to be unprofitable. &nbsp;During 2005, 2004 and 2003, sales of manufactured homes, cost of sales of manufactured homes, and selling expense are directly attributable to the sale of repossessed manufactured homes.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Rental income and reimbursements increased $2,045,877 or 72% for the year ended December 31, 2005 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2004. &nbsp;The increase is due to the additional rent and reimbursement income related to the property acquisitions made in 2005 in Tampa, Florida, Lakeland, Florida, Augusta, Georgia, and El Paso, Texas, and also a full year of rental income and reimbursements from the Richmond, Virginia, Quakertown, Pennsylvania, and Montgomery, Illinois properties purchased in 2004. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Interest and dividend income decreased $112,698 or 9% for the year ended December 31, 2005 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2004. &nbsp;The decrease was due mainly to a lower average balance of securities available for sale held during 2005 as compared to 2004. &nbsp;The average balance of securities was approximately $11,222,000 and $14,831,000 for the years ended December 31, 2005 and 2004, respectively. &nbsp;Weighted average yield of the securities portfolio was approximately 7.33% and 7.25% for the years ended December 31, 2005 and 2004, respectively. &nbsp;The balance of securities available for sale at December 31, 2005 and 2004 was $8,107,492 and $12,130,692, respectively.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR>
<BR></P>

<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Gain on securities transactions, net decreased $171,170 or 21% for the year ended December 31, 2005 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2004. The decrease is due to the Company taking advantage of the unrealized gains experienced in the portfolio during 2003 and 2004. &nbsp;The Company invests in securities as proxy for real property until suitable acquisitions are available and for additional income. The Company does not expect that it would be recording the same level of gain on sale of securities available for sale in future years that it recorded in 2004 due to the reduction in the securities portfolio occurring in 2005. &nbsp;The net gain on sale of securities of $619,619 was partially offset by the unrealized loss on futures contracts as of December 31, 2005 of $28,125. &nbsp;The Company invests in futures contracts of ten-year treasury notes as a way to mitigate the impact of rising interest rates on the Company&#146;s preferred equity and debt securities portfolios. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Other income decreased $244,123 for the year ended December 31, 2005 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2004. &nbsp;The decrease relates to the proceeds received during 2004 from the settlement of the lawsuit against the former owner of the Carlstadt, New Jersey property and the engineering firm used for due diligence. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Real estate taxes increased $177,747 or 47% for the year ended December 31, 2005 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2004. &nbsp;The increase relates to the real estate taxes related to the acquisitions of industrial properties made during 2004 and 2005. &nbsp;Since the properties are subject to net leases, real estate taxes are reimbursed by the tenants and included in reimbursement income above. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Professional fees decreased $40,498 or 25% for the year ended December 31, 2005 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2004. &nbsp;The decrease is due mainly to increased legal fees incurred during 2004 relating to the settled lawsuit mentioned above.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Interest expense increased $1,201,578 or 73% for the year ended December 31, 2005 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2004. &nbsp;The increase is due mainly to the interest related to the Debentures issued in March 2005 which mature in 2015 and the interest on the mortgages related to the new acquisitions of industrial property in 2004 and 2005. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Other expenses increased $182,358 or 32% for the year ended December 31, 2005 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2004. &nbsp;The increase is due mainly to increases in other expenses related the new acquisitions of industrial property, an increase in stock records </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-20-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>expenses for the Company&#146;s transfer from the NASDAQ small cap market to the NASDAQ national market and expanded operations. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Year Ended December 31, 2004 vs. Year Ended December 31, 2003</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Income is comprised primarily of rental income and reimbursements, interest and dividend income and gain on securities transactions. &nbsp;On March 30, 2001, the Company exited the manufactured home sales business since it proved to be unprofitable. &nbsp;During 2004 and 2003, sales of manufactured homes, cost of sales of manufactured homes, and selling expense are directly attributable to the sale of repossessed manufactured homes.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Rental income and reimbursements increased $1,101,031 or 63% for the year ended December 31, 2004 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2003. &nbsp;The increase is due to the additional rent and reimbursement income related to the property acquisitions made in 2004 in Richmond, Virginia, Quakertown, Pennsylvania, and Montgomery, Illinois and also a full year of rent and reimbursement income from the Wheeling, Illinois property purchased in August 2003.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Interest and dividend income increased $156,064 or 14% for the year ended December 31, 2004 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2003. &nbsp;The increase was due mainly to a higher average balance of securities available for sale held during 2004 as compared to 2003 partially offset by a decrease in the weighted average yield of the securities portfolio in 2004 as compared to 2003. &nbsp;The average balance of securities was approximately $14,831,273 and $11,035,892 for the years ended December 31, 2004 and 2003, respectively. &nbsp;Weighted average yield of the securities portfolio was 7.25% and 8.38% for the years ended December 31, 2004 and 2003, respectively. &nbsp;The balance of securities available for sale at December 31, 2004 and 2003 was $12,130,692 and $15,443,909, respectively.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Gain on securities transactions, net increased $105,353 or 15% for the year ended December 31, 2004 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2003. The increase in gain on sale is due to the Company taking advantage of the unrealized gains experienced in the portfolio during 2004. &nbsp;The Company invests in securities as proxy for real property until suitable acquisitions are available and for additional income. The Company does not expect that it would be recording the same level of gain on sale of securities available for sale in future years that it recorded in 2004 due to the reduction in the securities portfolio occurring in 2004. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The net gain on sale of securities of $958,743 was partially offset by the realized losses on futures contracts for the year ended December 31, 2004 of $147,586. &nbsp;During 2004, the Company began investing in futures contracts of ten-year treasury notes as a way to mitigate the impact of rising interest rates on the Company&#146;s preferred equity and debt securities portfolios. At December 31, 2004, the Company had unrealized gains on open futures contracts of $4,687 which is also included in gain on securities transactions, net. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-21-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Other income increased $243,129 for the year ended December 31, 2004 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2003. &nbsp;The increase relates to the proceeds received from the lawsuit against the former owner of the Carlstadt, New Jersey property and the engineering firm used for due diligence. &nbsp;Professional fees increased for the year ended three December 31, 2004 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2003 due mainly to increased legal fees related to this settlement.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Real estate taxes increased $265,202 for the year ended December 31, 2004 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2003. &nbsp;The increase relates to the real estate taxes related to the acquisitions, mainly the Wheeling, Illinois property. &nbsp;Since the properties are subject to net leases, real estate taxes are reimbursed by the tenants. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Salaries and benefits increased $62,053 or 35% for the year ended December 31, 2004 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2003. &nbsp;&nbsp;The increase is due mainly to salary increases, salaries related to employees who were hired during 2003, and minor changes in allocations of employee related costs from the related company MREIC.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Professional fees increased $91,543 or 127% for the year ended December 31, 2004 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2003. &nbsp;The increase is due mainly to legal fees related to the lawsuit against the former owner of the Carlstadt, New Jersey property and the engineering firm used for due diligence &nbsp;and from potential acquisitions which were abandoned.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Interest expense increased $658,480 or 67% for the year ended December 31, 2004 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2003. &nbsp;The increase is due mainly to the interest related to the Debentures issued in October 2003 and mortgages related to the new acquisitions in 2003 and 2004. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Depreciation expense increased $458,167 or 140% for the year ended December 31, 2004 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2003 and other expenses increased $78,818 or 16% for the year ended December 31, 2004 as compared to the year ended December 31, 2003. &nbsp;The increases are due mainly to the new acquisitions in 2003 and 2004.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>

<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><B><U>Liquidity and Capital Resources and Changes in Financial Condition</U></B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company&#146;s ability to generate cash adequate to meet its needs is dependent primarily on income from its real estate investments and its securities portfolio, the sale of real estate investments and securities, refinancing of mortgage debt, leveraging of real estate investments, availability of bank borrowings, proceeds from the Dividend Reinvestment and Stock Purchase Plan, proceeds from the Subordinated Convertible Debentures, and access to the capital markets. &nbsp;Purchases of new properties, purchases of securities, payments of expenses related to real estate operations, capital improvements programs, debt service, management and professional fees, and dividend requirements place demands on the Company&#146;s liquidity.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company intends to operate its existing properties from the cash flows generated by the properties. &nbsp;However, the Company&#146;s expenses are affected by various factors, including inflation. &nbsp;Increases in operating expenses raise the breakeven point for a property and, to the extent that they cannot be passed on through higher rents, reduce the amount of available cash flow which can adversely affect the market value of the property.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Net cash provided by operating activities for the years ended December 31, 2005, 2004, and 2003 was $1,768,790, $1,428,602 and $898,188, respectively. &nbsp;The increase is due primarily to profitable and expanded operations. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Net cash flows used in investing activities for the years ended December 31, 2005, 2004 and 2003 was $14,537,039, $13,085,511 and $13,190,104, respectively. &nbsp;The increase was due mainly to purchases of industrial properties. &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company intends to grow its real estate investment portfolio. &nbsp;&nbsp;During the past five years, the Company purchased eleven warehouse facilities at an aggregate cost of approximately $59,000,000. &nbsp;The Company financed these purchases primarily through mortgages on its acquisitions and short term bank loans. &nbsp;The Company expects to make additional real estate acquisitions from time to time. &nbsp;&nbsp;During 2006 the Company plans to acquire approximately $20,000,000 in properties. &nbsp;The funds for these acquisitions may come from mortgages secured for the acquisitions, other bank borrowings, proceeds from the Dividend Reinvestment and Stock Purchase Plan, proceeds from private placements, or proceeds from sales of the Company&#146;s investment securities. &nbsp;To the extent funds or appropriate properties are not available, fewer acquisitions will be made. &nbsp;Funds generated are expected to be sufficient to meet debt service requirements and capital expenditures of the Company. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Total real estate investments, net of accumulated depreciation, increased $15,554,496 during the year ended December 31, 2005. &nbsp;This was due primarily to the purchase of four warehouse facilities located in Tampa, Florida, Lakeland, Florida, Augusta, Georgia and El Paso, Texas, partially offset by depreciation for the year. &nbsp;Intangible assets, net increased $1,900,941 due to the value assigned to the leases related to the acquisition of the warehouse facilities noted above, offset by the current year amortization. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-24-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company also invests in debt and equity securities of other REIT&#146;s as a proxy for real estate when suitable acquisitions are not available, for liquidity, and for additional income. &nbsp;&nbsp;The Company from time to time may purchase these securities on margin when there is adequate yield spread. &nbsp;&nbsp;At December 31, 2005, the securities portfolio balance was $8,107,492 (with $-0- margin debt). &nbsp;During 2005, the Company&#146;s security portfolio decreased by $4,023,200 due to sale, redemptions and returns of principal of $8,346,135 a decrease in the unrealized gain of $1,108,562 partially offset by purchases of $5,431,497. &nbsp;During 2005, the Company sold or redeemed proceeds of $8,990,809, recognizing a gain on securities transactions, net of $644,674. &nbsp;The Company anticipates that it may make additional investments in REIT securities if funds are available and suitable acquisitions of properties are not available. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Loans receivable relate to the financing of manufactured home sales when the Company was engaged in the manufactured mobile home sales business. Loans receivable decreased by $277,379 during the year ended December 31, 2005. &nbsp;This decrease was primarily due to collections of $172,339. The Company also repossessed the collateral for loans receivable of $105,040 and placed the homes into inventory. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Cash flows from financing activities for the years ended December 31, 2005, 2004 and 2003 were $13,974,554, $11,876,504 and $12,431,908, respectively. &nbsp;&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Mortgages payable increased $9,313,831 due mainly to the new mortgages of $10,175,000 related to the new acquisitions of industrial properties offset by principal payments on mortgages of $861,169. &nbsp;Loans payable decreased $8,749,156 due mainly to the payoff of the $2,500,000 short term loan originated with Two River Community Bank (Two River) and net payments on the Company&#146;s margin loan. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Financing costs, net and leasing costs, net increased by $649,469 primarily as a result of the purchase of the industrial buildings during 2005. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Shareholders&#146; equity increased from $15,446,093 as of December 31, 2004 to $18,439,983 as of December 31, 2005. &nbsp;&nbsp;The Company has a Dividend Reinvestment and Stock Purchase Plan (the DRIP) in which participants purchase stock from the Company at a discount of approximately 5% of market price. &nbsp;During 2005, a total of $4,948,282 in additional capital was raised through the DRIP. &nbsp;The success of the DRIP has resulted in substantial improvement in the Company&#146;s liquidity and capital resources during 2005. &nbsp;It is anticipated, although no assurances can be given, that a comparable level of participation will continue in the DRIP in 2006. &nbsp;Two employees exercised their stock options during 2005 and 20,000 shares were issued for a total of $52,500. &nbsp;One holder of the 2013 Debentures converted $50,000 in Debentures to 8,333 shares of common stock. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-25-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><B><U>Recent Accounting Pronouncements</U></B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>On April 14, 2005 the Securities and Exchange Commission amended the compliance dates for the Financial Accounting Standard Board&#146;s (FASB) Statement of Financial Accounting Standards No. 123 (revision 2004), Share-Based Payment (Statement No. 123R). &nbsp;The Commission&#146;s new rule allows companies to implement Statement No. 123R at the beginning of the next fiscal year, instead of the next reporting period, that begins after June 15, 2005, or December 15, 2005 for small business issuers. &nbsp;The Commission&#146;s new rule does not change the accounting required by Statement No. 123R; it changes only the dates for compliance with the standard. &nbsp;The Company has evaluated the impact of implementation of Statement No. 123R and does not believe that it will be material.</P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>In May of 2005, the FASB issued Statement of Financial Accounting Standard No.&nbsp;154, Accounting Changes and Error Correction (Statement No. 154). &nbsp;This standard is a replacement of Accounting Policy Board Opinion No.&nbsp;20 and FASB Standard No.&nbsp;3. &nbsp;Under the new standard, any voluntary changes in accounting principles should be adopted via a retrospective application of the accounting principle in the financial statements presented in addition to obtaining an opinion from the auditors that the new principle is preferred. &nbsp;In addition, adoption of a change in accounting principle required by the issuance of a new accounting standard would also require retroactive restatement, unless the new standard includes explicit transition guidelines. &nbsp;This new standard is effective for fiscal years beginning after December&nbsp;14, 2005. &nbsp;The Company has evaluated the impact of implementation of Statement No. 154 and does not believe that it will be material.</P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>In June 2005, the FASB issued Emerging Issues Task Force (EITF) No. 04-05, Determining Whether a General Partner, or the General Partners as a Group, Controls a Limited Partnership or Similar Entity When Limited Partners Have Certain Rights. &nbsp;EITF No. 04-05 replaces counterpart requirements in SOP 78-9, which provides guidance on accounting for investments in real estate ventures and limited partnerships. &nbsp;Under EITF No. 04-05, the general partner&#146;s control of a venture would be overcome if the limited partners have either &#147;kick-out rights&#148; (the right to dissolve or liquidate the venture or otherwise remove the general partner &#147;without cause&#148;) or &#147;participating rights&#148; (the right to effectively participate in significant decisions made in the ordinary course of the ventures business. &nbsp;The adoption of EITF 04-05 has not had a material effect on the consolidated financial statements. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-26-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><B><U>Safe Harbor Statement</U></B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>This Form 10-K contains various &#147;forward-looking statements&#148; within the meaning of the Securities Act of 1933 and the Securities Exchange Act of 1934, and the Company intends that such forward-looking statements be subject to the safe harbors created thereby. &nbsp;The words &#147;may&#148;, &#147;will&#148;, &#147;expect&#148;, &#147;believe&#148;, &#147;anticipate&#148;, &#147;should&#148;, &#147;estimate&#148;, and similar expressions identify forward-looking statements. &nbsp;These forward looking statements reflect the Company&#146;s current views with respect to future events and financial performance, but are based upon current assumptions regarding the &nbsp;&nbsp;Company&#146;s &nbsp;&nbsp;operations, &nbsp;future results and prospects, and are subject to many uncertainties and factors relating to the Company&#146;s operations and business environment which may cause the actual results of the Company to be materially different from any future results expressed or implied by such forward-looking statements.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Such factors include, but are not limited to, the following: (i) changes in the general economic climate, including interest rates; (ii) increased competition in the geographic areas in which the Company operates; and (iii) changes in government laws. &nbsp;The Company undertakes no obligation to publicly update or revise any forward-looking statements whether as a result of new information, future events, or otherwise. </P>

<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>ITEM 7A &#150; QUANTITATIVE AND QUALITATIVE DISCLOSURES ABOUT MARKET RISK</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company is exposed to interest rate changes primarily as a result of its long-term debt used to maintain liquidity and fund capital expenditures and expansion of the Company&#146;s real estate investment portfolio and operations. &nbsp;The Company&#146;s interest rate risk management objectives are to limit the impact of interest rate changes on earnings and cash flows and to lower its overall borrowing costs. &nbsp;To achieve its objectives, the Company borrows primarily at fixed rates. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Below is the information as of December 31, 2005, concerning the Company&#146;s fixed-rate debt obligations, including principal cash flow by scheduled maturity, weighted average interest rates, and estimated fair value:</P>

<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>In addition to the mortgages, the Company has $760,998 carrying value in a line of credit with Two River. &nbsp;The line of credit is at a variable rate of 8.25% as of December 31, 2005, and expires in 2011. &nbsp;The Company has no outstanding balance on its margin loans as of December 31, 2005. &nbsp;The margin loans are secured by marketable securities. &nbsp;The interest rate on these margin loans ranged from 4% to 6% during 2005. &nbsp;&nbsp;The<FONT FACE="Courier New"> </FONT>carrying value of the Company&#146;s variable rate debt approximates fair value as of December 31, 2005.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company also invests in both debt and equity securities of other REITs and is primarily exposed to equity price risk from adverse changes in market rates and conditions. &nbsp;All securities are classified as available for sale and are carried at fair value. &nbsp;The Company has no significant interest rate risk relating to debt securities as they are short-term in nature. &nbsp;&nbsp;To reduce exposure of the preferred equity securities portfolio to interest rate fluctuations, the Company invests in futures contracts of ten-year treasury notes. &nbsp;These contracts are marked to market which changes in market recognized in income.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>

<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>ITEM 9A &#150; CONTROLS AND PROCEDURES</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company&#146;s President and Chief Financial Officer, with the assistance of other members of the Company&#146;s management, have evaluated the effectiveness of the Company&#146;s disclosure controls and procedures as of the end of the period covered by this Annual Report on Form 10-K. &nbsp;Based on such evaluation, the Company&#146;s President and Chief Financial Officer have concluded that the Company&#146;s disclosure controls and procedures are effective.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company&#146;s President and Chief Financial Officer have also concluded that there have not been any changes in the Company&#146;s internal control over financial reporting that has materially affected, or is reasonably likely to materially affect, the Company&#146;s internal control over financial reporting.</P>

<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt"><U>Audit Committee</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:72pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company has a separately-designated standing audit committee established in accordance with section 3 (a)(58)(A) of the Exchange Act (15 U.S.C. 78c(a)(58)(A)). &nbsp;The members of the audit committee are Stephen B. Wolgin (Chairman), Neal Herstik and Eugene Rothenberg. &nbsp;The Company&#146;s Board of Directors has determined that Stephen B. Wolgin is a financial expert. &nbsp;</P>
