<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Compensation of Directors</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Directors receive a fee of $800 for each Board meeting attended. &nbsp;Directors appointed to house committees receive $150 for each meeting attended. &nbsp;Those specific committees are Compensation Committee, Audit Committee and Stock Option Committee.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-33-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt"><U>Report of the Compensation Committee</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt"><I>Overview and Philosophy</I></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=center><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company has a Compensation Committee consisting of three independent outside Directors. &nbsp;This Committee is responsible for making recommendations to the Board of Directors concerning executive compensation. The Compensation Committee takes into consideration three major factors in setting compensation.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The first consideration is the overall performance of the Company. The Committee believes that the financial interests of the executive officers should be aligned with the success of the Company and the financial interests of its shareholders.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The second consideration is the individual achievements made by each officer. &nbsp;The Company is relatively small. &nbsp;The Committee is aware of the contributions made by each officer and makes an evaluation of individual performance based on the Committee&#146;s own familiarity with the officer.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The final criteria in setting compensation are comparable wages in the industry. &nbsp;In this regard, the REIT industry maintains excellent statistics.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><I>Evaluation</I></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Committee reviewed the progress made by Eugene W. Landy, Chief Executive Officer, in locating alternative business and investment opportunities. The Committee decided to continue Mr. Landy's annual compensation of $50,000. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:288pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Compensation Committee:</P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:540pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Eugene D. Rothenberg</P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:324pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Robert G. Sampson</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt"><U>Other Information</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Except for specific agreements, the Company has no retirement plan in effect for officers, directors or employees and, at present, has no intention of instituting such a plan.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>

<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Audit fees include professional services rendered by Cowan Gunteski for the audit of the Company&#146;s annual financial statements and reviews of financial statements included in the Company&#146;s quarterly reports on Form 10-Q. &nbsp;Audit fees also include services that are normally provided by the Company&#146;s independent registered public accounting firm in connection with statutory and regulatory filings, such as consents and assistance with and review of documents filed with the Securities and Exchange Commission.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Tax fees include professional services rendered by Cowan Gunteski for the preparation of the Company&#146;s federal and state corporate tax returns and supporting schedules as may be required by the Internal Revenue Service and applicable state taxing authorities. &nbsp;Tax fees also include other work directly affecting or supporting the payment of taxes, including planning and research of various tax issues.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Other fees include professional services rendered by Cowan Gunteski for consulting on software. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><B>Audit Committee Pre-Approval Policy</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Audit Committee has adopted a policy for the pre-approval of audit and permitted non-audit services provided by the Company&#146;s principal independent accountants. &nbsp;The policy requires that all services provided by Cowan Gunteski to the Company, including audit services, audit-related services, tax services and other services, must be pre-approved by the Committee. &nbsp;The pre-approval requirements do not prohibit day-to-day normal tax consulting services, which matters will not exceed $5,000 in the aggregate. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Audit Committee has determined that the provision of the non-audit services described above is compatible with maintaining Cowan Gunteski&#146;s independence.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>

<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-40-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>

<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=center><B><U>REPORT OF INDEPENDENT REGISTERED PUBLIC ACCOUNTING FIRM</U></B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>To the Board of Directors and Shareholders </P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Monmouth Capital Corporation</P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Freehold, New Jersey</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>We have audited the accompanying consolidated balance sheets of Monmouth Capital Corporation as of December 31, 2005 and 2004, and the related consolidated statements of income, shareholders' equity and cash flows for each of the three years in the period ended December 31, 2005. &nbsp;These consolidated financial statements are the responsibility of the Company's management. &nbsp;Our responsibility is to express an opinion on these financial statements based on our audits.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>We conducted our audits in accordance with the standards of the Public Company Accounting Oversight Board (United States). &nbsp;Those standards require that we plan and perform the audit to obtain reasonable assurance about whether the consolidated financial statements are free of material misstatement. &nbsp;An audit includes examining, on a test basis, evidence supporting the amounts and disclosures in the consolidated financial statements. &nbsp;An audit also includes assessing the accounting principles used and significant estimates made by management, as well as evaluating the overall financial statement presentation. We believe that our audits provide a reasonable basis for our opinion.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>In our opinion, the consolidated financial statements referred to above present fairly, in all material respects, the financial position of Monmouth Capital Corporation as of December 31, 2005 and 2004, and the consolidated results of their operations and their cash flows for each of the three years in the period ended December 31, 2005, in conformity with U.S. generally accepted accounting principles.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:180pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>/s/ Cowan, Gunteski &amp; Co., P.A.</P>

<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt">*</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=center><B>MONMOUTH CAPITAL CORPORATION</B> </P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=center><B>NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS </B></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=center><B>DECEMBER 31, 2005</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-right:-31.5pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>NOTE 1 &#150; ORGANIZATION AND SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT ACCOUNTING POLICIES </U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Monmouth Capital Corporation is a corporation organized in the State of New Jersey, in 1961. &nbsp;The Company operates as a qualified real estate investment trust (REIT) under Sections 856-860 of the Internal Revenue Code (the Code) and intends to maintain its qualification as a REIT in the future. &nbsp;As a qualified REIT, with limited exceptions, the Company will not be taxed under federal and certain state income tax laws at the corporate level on taxable income that it distributes to its shareholders. &nbsp;For special tax provisions applicable to REITS, refer to Sections 856-860 of the Code. &nbsp;The Company is subject to franchise taxes in some of the states in which the Company owns property.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Prior to 1994 the Company operated as a small business investment company. &nbsp;During 1994, the Company formed a wholly-owned subsidiary, The Mobile Home Store, Inc. (MHS), to finance and sell manufactured homes. &nbsp;The sales operation was conducted at manufactured home communities owned by United Mobile Homes, Inc. (United), a related REIT. &nbsp;&nbsp;The Company also invested in real estate and securities.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>In March 2001, the Company sold the existing inventory to United at the Company&#146;s carrying value and the Company exited the manufactured home sales business since it proved to be unprofitable. &nbsp;&nbsp;On September 26, 2001, the Company adopted a change from a fiscal year end of March 31 to a calendar year end, effective for the short year ended December 31, 2001. &nbsp;The Company elected to be taxed as a real estate investment trust for the transition period ended December 31, 2001. &nbsp;MHS was merged into the Company on December 6, 2001. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company has made the following property acquisitions in the last five years:</P>

<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:72pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>On February 8, 2005, the Company formed a wholly-owned taxable REIT subsidiary organized in Maryland, named MCC Financial, Inc. &nbsp;MCC Financial, Inc. had no activity during the year ended December 31, 2005, other than holding $500,000 in certificates of deposit.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-47-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company is currently operating as a diversified REIT investing in real estate equities, mortgages, mortgage-backed securities, and other REIT securities. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt"><U>Principals of Consolidation and Minority Interests</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The consolidated financial statements of the Company at December 31, 2005, 2004, and 2003 and for each of the years ended December 31, 2005, 2004, and 2003 include the accounts and operating results of the Company and its subsidiaries. &nbsp;The Company consolidates the results of operations that have minority interests. &nbsp;Such consolidated financial statements present the Company&#146;s minority interests under the equity method of accounting. &nbsp;All intercompany balances and transactions have been eliminated in consolidation.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Use of Estimates</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The preparation of the financial statements in conformity with generally accepted accounting principles accepted in the United States of America requires management to make estimates and assumptions that affect the amounts reported in the financial statements and accompanying notes. &nbsp;Actual results could differ from those estimates.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Buildings, Improvements and Equipment</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Buildings, Improvements and Equipment are stated at the lower of depreciated cost or net realizable value. &nbsp;Depreciation is computed using accelerated depreciation methods acceptable for tax reporting purposes over the estimated useful life of the assets (ranging from 5 to 39 years). &nbsp;This departure from GAAP does not create a material difference to the Company&#146;s operating income based on its REIT election. &nbsp;See Note 12 for a discussion of the Company&#146;s REIT status.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Acquisitions</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company records direct costs and deposits associated with potential acquisitions to Other Assets. &nbsp;Upon closing of the acquisition, the costs are reclassified to real estate investments. &nbsp;The costs are expensed if the acquisition is not consummated.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:45.8pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Upon acquisition of a property, the Company allocates the purchase price of the property based upon the fair value of the assets acquired, which generally consist of land, buildings, leasing commissions and intangible assets, including in-place leases and above and below market leases. The Company allocates the purchase price to the fair value of the tangible assets of an acquired property determined by third party appraisal of the property obtained in conjunction with the purchase. Acquired above and below market leases are valued based on the present value of the difference between prevailing market rates and the in-place rates over the remaining lease term. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:45.8pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The purchase price is further allocated to in-place lease values based on management's evaluation of the specific characteristics of each tenant's lease and the Company's overall relationship with the respective tenant. &nbsp;Acquired above and below market leases are amortized over the remaining non-cancelable terms of the respective leases. The value of in-place lease intangibles is amortized to expense over the remaining lease term. &nbsp;If a tenant terminates its lease early, the unamortized portion of the tenant improvements, leasing commissions above and below market leases and the in-place lease value is immediately charged to expense.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-48-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Securities Available for Sale</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company's securities consist primarily of debt, and common and preferred equities of other REITs, as well as mortgaged backed securities issued by the Federal National Mortgage Association and the Government National Mortgage Association. &nbsp;These securities are all publicly traded and purchased on the open market. &nbsp;These securities are classified as Available-for-Sale, and are carried at fair value based upon quoted market prices. Gains or losses on the sale of securities are calculated on the average cost method and are accounted for on a trade date basis. &nbsp;Unrealized holding gains and losses are excluded from earnings and reported as a separate component of Shareholders' Equity until realized. &nbsp;A decline in the market value of any security below cost that is deemed to be other than temporary results in a reduction in the carrying amount to fair value. &nbsp;Any impairment is charged to earnings and a new cost basis for the security is established.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt"><U>Derivative Financial Instruments</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company invests in futures contracts of ten-year treasury notes to reduce exposure of the debt and preferred equities securities portfolios to interest rate fluctuations. &nbsp;These futures contracts do not qualify for hedge accounting under Statement of Financial Accounting Standards (SFAS) No. 133, Accounting for Derivative Instruments and Hedging Activities, as amended by SFAS No. 138 and No. 149. &nbsp;The open contracts are marked-to-market and the unrealized gain or loss is recorded in the income statement in gain on securities transactions, net with corresponding amounts recorded in other assets or other liabilities on the balance sheet. &nbsp;Gain or loss on settled futures contracts are also recorded as a component of gain on securities transactions, net. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt"><U>Cash and Cash Equivalents</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Cash and cash equivalents include all cash and investments with an original maturity of three months or less. &nbsp;The Company maintains its cash in bank accounts in amounts that may exceed federally insured limits. &nbsp;The Company has not experienced any losses in these accounts in the past and does not believe that it is exposed to significant credit risk. The fair value of cash and cash equivalents approximates their current carrying amounts since all such items are short-term in nature.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt"><U>Loans Receivable</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Interest income on loans receivable is accrued until, in the opinion of management, the collection of such interest appears doubtful. &nbsp;An allowance is recorded when it appears doubtful that the Company will collect the full principal amount. &nbsp;The fair value of loans receivable approximates its current carrying amounts since all such items are short-term in nature.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-49-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt"><U>Intangible Assets, Lease Costs and Financing Costs</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Intangible assets, consisting primarily of the value of in-place leases, are amortized to expense over the remaining terms of the respective leases. &nbsp;Upon termination of a lease, the unamortized portion is immediately charged to expense. &nbsp;Costs incurred in connection with the execution of leases are deferred and are amortized over the term of the respective leases. &nbsp;Unamortized lease costs are charged to expense upon cancellation of leases prior to the expiration of lease terms. &nbsp;Costs incurred in connection with obtaining mortgages and other financings and refinancings are deferred and are amortized over the term of the related obligations. &nbsp;Unamortized costs are charged to expense upon prepayment of the obligation. &nbsp;Costs incurred in connection with the Convertible Subordinated Debentures (the Debentures) are deferred and are amortized over the term of the Debentures. &nbsp;Unamortized costs are charged pro-rata to Additional Paid-In Capital upon conversion by the Debenture holders or redemption by the Company.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Inventories</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Inventories, consisting of manufactured homes for sale, are valued at the lower of cost, which includes costs associated with the repossession of a home, or market value and are determined by the specific identification method. &nbsp;All inventories were considered finished goods.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt"><U>Prepaid Expenses and Other Assets</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt">The fair value of prepaid expenses and other assets approximates their carrying value due to the short-term nature. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt"><U>Mortgages Payable, Loans Payable and Convertible Subordinated Debentures</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>At December 31, 2005, the fair and carrying value of mortgages payable was $31,764,700 and $30,977,186, respectively. &nbsp;At December 31, 2004, the fair and carrying value of mortgages payable was $22,230,800 and $21,663,355, respectively. &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The fair value of loans payable approximates their current carrying amounts since such amounts payable are at a current market rate of interest. &nbsp;The fair value of the convertible subordinated debentures approximates their current carrying amounts since such amounts payable are at a current market rate of interest for similar convertible debentures. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Revenue Recognition</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Rental income is recognized on the straight-line basis over the term of the lease. &nbsp;The fair value of deferred rent receivable approximates its current carrying amounts. Sale of manufactured homes is recognized on the full accrual basis when certain criteria are met. Interest income on loans receivable is not accrued when, in the opinion of management, the collection of such interest appears doubtful.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-50-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Income Taxes</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company has elected to be taxed as a REIT under Sections 856-860 of the Internal Revenue Code. &nbsp;The Company will not be taxed on the portion of its income which is distributed to shareholders, provided it distributes at least 90% of its taxable income, has at least 75% of its assets in real estate investments and meets certain other requirements for qualification as a REIT.</P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Additionally, at April 1, 2001, the Company had certain assets with built-in gains. &nbsp;If these assets are sold within a ten-year period, any gain may be taxable.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Net Income Per Share</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Basic net income per share is calculated by dividing net income by the weighted-average number of common shares outstanding during the period (4,142,355, 3,362,018 and 2,824,809 for the year ended December 31, 2005, 2004, and 2003, respectively). &nbsp;Diluted net income per share is calculated by dividing net income plus interest expense related to the Debentures by the weighted-average number of common shares outstanding plus the weighted-average number of net shares that would be issued upon exercise of stock options pursuant to the treasury stock method, plus the number of shares resulting from the possible conversion of the Debentures, (4,175,929, 3,404,950 and 3,747,584 for the years ended December 31, 2005, 2004, and 2003, respectively). &nbsp;&nbsp;(See Note 6). &nbsp;Options in the amount of 33,574, 42,932 and 27,775 are included in the diluted weighted average shares outstanding for the year ended December 31, 2005, 2004, and 2003, respectively. &nbsp;Common stock relating to the Company&#146;s Debentures totaling 895,000 shares is also included in the diluted weighted average shares outstanding for the year ended December 31, 2003. &nbsp;Interest expense and common stock related to the Debentures are excluded, from the calculation of diluted net income per share for the year ended December 31, 3005 and 2004, due to their antidilutive effect.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Stock Option Plan</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company adopted the fair value recognition provisions of SFAS No. 123, &#147;Accounting for Stock Based Compensation&#148; on January 1, 2003. &nbsp;Under the prospective method of adoption selected by the Company under the provisions of SFAS No. 148, &#147;Accounting for Stock Based Compensation, Transition and Disclosure&#148;, compensation costs of $13,325, -0-, and -0- have been recognized in 2005, 2004 and 2003, respectively.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Comprehensive Income</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Comprehensive income consists of net income and net unrealized gains or losses on securities available for sale and is presented in the consolidated statements of shareholders&#146; equity.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Reclassifications</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Certain amounts in the consolidated financial statements for the prior periods have been reclassified to conform to the statement presentation for the current year. &nbsp;These reclassifications have no effect on net income.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-51-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Fair Value</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company is required to disclose certain information about fair values of financial instruments, as defined in Statement of Financial Accounting Standards No. 107, &#147;Disclosures About Fair Value of Financial Instruments&#148;.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><I>Limitations</I></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Estimates of fair value are made at a specific point in time based upon where available, relevant market prices and information about the financial instrument. Such estimates do not include any premium or discount that could result from offering for sale at one time the Company&#146;s entire holdings of a particular financial instrument. &nbsp;All of the Company&#146;s securities available for sale have quoted market values. &nbsp;However, for a portion of the Company&#146;s financial instruments, no quoted market value exists. Therefore, estimates of fair value are necessarily based on a number of significant assumptions (many of which involve events outside the control of management). &nbsp;Such assumptions include assessments of current economic conditions, perceived risks associated with these financial instruments and their counterparties, future expected loss experience and other factors. &nbsp;Given the uncertainties surrounding these assumptions, the reported fair values represent estimates only, and, therefore, cannot be compared to the historical accounting model. Use of different assumptions or methodologies is likely to result in significantly different fair value estimates.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-52-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Recent Accounting Pronouncements</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>On April 14, 2005 the Securities and Exchange Commission amended the compliance dates for the Financial Accounting Standard Board&#146;s (FASB) Statement of Financial Accounting Standards No. 123 (revision 2004), Share-Based Payment (Statement No. 123R). &nbsp;The Commission&#146;s new rule allows companies to implement Statement No. 123R at the beginning of the next fiscal year, instead of the next reporting period, that begins after June 15, 2005, or December 15, 2005 for small business issuers. &nbsp;The Commission&#146;s new rule does not change the accounting required by Statement No. 123R; it changes only the dates for compliance with the standard. &nbsp;The Company has evaluated the impact of implementation of Statement No. 123R and does not believe that it will be material.</P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>In May of 2005, the FASB issued Statement of Financial Accounting Standard No.&nbsp;154, Accounting Changes and Error Correction (Statement No. 154). &nbsp;This standard is a replacement of Accounting Policy Board Opinion No.&nbsp;20 and FASB Standard No.&nbsp;3. &nbsp;Under the new standard, any voluntary changes in accounting principles should be adopted via a retrospective application of the accounting principle in the financial statements presented in addition to obtaining an opinion from the auditors that the new principle is preferred. &nbsp;In addition, adoption of a change in accounting principle required by the issuance of a new accounting standard would also require retroactive restatement, unless the new standard includes explicit transition guidelines. &nbsp;This new standard is effective for fiscal years beginning after December&nbsp;14, 2005. &nbsp;The Company has evaluated the impact of implementation of Statement No. 154 and does not believe that it will be material.</P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>&nbsp;</P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>In June 2005, the FASB issued Emerging Issues Task Force (EITF) No. 04-05, Determining Whether a General Partner, or the General Partners as a Group, Controls a Limited Partnership or Similar Entity When Limited Partners Have Certain Rights. &nbsp;EITF No. 04-05 replaces counterpart requirements in SOP 78-9, which provides guidance on accounting for investments in real estate ventures and limited partnerships. &nbsp;Under EITF No. 04-05, the general partner&#146;s control of a venture would be overcome if the limited partners have either &#147;kick-out rights&#148; (the right to dissolve or liquidate the venture or otherwise remove the general partner &#147;without cause&#148;) or &#147;participating rights&#148; (the right to effectively participate in significant decisions made in the ordinary course of the ventures business. &nbsp;The adoption of EITF 04-05 has not had a material effect on the consolidated financial statements. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-53-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>

<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>NOTE 3 - ACQUISITIONS</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>On February 24, 2005, the Company purchased a 68,385 a square foot industrial building in Tampa, Florida. &nbsp;This warehouse facility is 100% net leased to Kellogg Sales Co. for 20 years. &nbsp;The lease expires December 31, 2009. &nbsp;The average annual rent over the term of the lease is $444,000. &nbsp;The purchase price, including closing costs, was approximately $5,600,000. &nbsp;The Company obtained $4,000,000 in mortgages at an interest rate of 5.71% on $3,400,000 of principal, due March 1, 2015 and 5.24% on $600,000 of principal, due March 1, 2010. &nbsp;In addition, the Company obtained $1,500,000 from its margin loan. &nbsp;Management estimated that the value allocated to the lease in-place at purchase was approximately $30,000.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>On November 8, 2005, the Company purchased a 31,096 square foot industrial building in Lakeland, Florida. &nbsp;This warehouse facility is 100% net leased to Federal Express Corporation for a period of seven remaining years though November 30, 2012. &nbsp;The average annual rent over the term of the lease is $165,000. &nbsp;The purchase price, including closing costs, was approximately $2,150,000. &nbsp;The Company obtained approximately $2,100,000 from its margin loan. &nbsp;Management estimated that the value allocated to the lease in-place at purchase was approximately $181,000.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-54-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>On November 8, 2005, the Company purchased a 30,332 square foot industrial building in Augusta, Georgia. &nbsp;This warehouse facility is 100% net leased to Federal Express Corporation for a period of seven remaining years though November 30, 2012. &nbsp;The average annual rent over the term of the lease is $141,500. &nbsp;The purchase price, including closing costs, was approximately $1,900,000. &nbsp;The Company obtained approximately $761,000 from its line of credit with Two River Bank and $1,039,000 from its margin loan. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:72pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>On December 9, 2005, Jones EPI, LLC, a Delaware limited liability company (Jones EPI), in which the Company holds a 65% equity interest, purchased a 91,854 square foot industrial building in El Paso, Texas. &nbsp;The warehouse facility is 100% net leased to FedEx Ground Package Systems for a period of ten years remaining through September 30, 2015. &nbsp;The average annual rent over the term of the lease is $667,600 (of which the Company receives 65% or $433,900). &nbsp;The purchase price, including closing costs, was approximately $9,000,000. &nbsp;Jones EPI obtained a mortgage of $6,175,000 at an interest rate of 5.4%, due January 5, 2017. &nbsp;The Company obtained approximately $1,836,000 from its margin loan for its 65% equity investment in Jones EPI. &nbsp;Management estimated that the value allocated to the lease in-place at purchase was approximately $1,710,000.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>NOTE 4 - LOANS RECEIVABLE</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>

<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>From 1994 through March 2001, the Company sold and financed manufactured home units. &nbsp;At December 31, 2005 and 2004, financed manufactured homes consist of 45 and 57 loans, respectively. These loans range from approximately $1,000 to approximately $60,000. &nbsp;Loans receivable for financed manufactured homes are secured by the property financed. Generally, the terms of the loans do not exceed 20 years.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>

<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>NOTE 5 &#150; SECURITIES AVAILABLE FOR SALE</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>

<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Convertible Subordinated Debentures</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>On October 23, 2003, the Company completed a private placement offering of $5,370,000 (less approximately $374,000 in offering costs) of 8% Convertible Subordinated Debentures, due 2013 (the 2013 Debentures). &nbsp;Interest is paid semi-annually in arrears on April 30 and October 31 of each year, commencing April 30, 2004. &nbsp;The 2013 Debentures are convertible into common stock of the Company at any time prior to redemption or maturity, at the conversion price of $6.00 per share (equivalent to a rate of 166.67 shares of common stock for each $1,000 principal amount), subject to adjustment under certain conditions. &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company may redeem the 2013 Debentures, at its option, in whole or in part, at any time on and after October 31, 2004 at the redemption prices set below. &nbsp;The redemption price, expressed as a percentage of the principal amount, is as follows for the 12-month periods beginning on:</P>

<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>No sinking fund is provided for the 2013 Debentures. &nbsp;The Company may redeem the debentures, at our option, in whole or in part, at any time prior to October 23, 2004, upon at least 30 and not more than 60 days&#146; notice by mail to the holders of the 2013 Debentures, at a redemption price equal to 100% of the principal amount of the debentures to be redeemed, plus accrued and unpaid interest to the date fixed for redemption, if the closing price of our common stock has exceeded 150% of the conversion price for at least 20 trading days in the consecutive 30-day trading period ending on the trading day prior to the date we mail the notice of redemption.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>One 2013 Debenture holder converted $50,000 of 2013 Debentures into 8,333 shares of common stock during the year ended December 31, 2005. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>On March 30, 2005, the Company completed a private placement offering of $10,420,000 (less approximately $456,000 in offering costs) of 8% Convertible Subordinated Debentures, due 2015 (the 2015 Debentures). &nbsp;Interest will be paid semi-annually in arrears on April 30 and October 31 of each year, commencing October 31, 2005. &nbsp;The 2015 Debentures are convertible into common stock of the Company at any time prior to redemption or maturity, at the conversion price of $7.50 per share (equivalent to a rate of 133.3333 shares of common stock for each $1,000 principal amount), subject to adjustment under certain conditions. &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;</P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>

<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Loans Payable</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>On April 15, 2004, the Company closed on a $1,000,000 line of credit with Two River Community Bank (Two River) secured by the Company&#146;s manufactured home loans. &nbsp;The interest rate is 5% for the first two years and changes to the five-year FHLB of NY Regular Fixed Rate plus 300bp for the remaining five years. &nbsp;The line of credit expires in 2011. &nbsp;The balance on the line of credit at December 31, 2005 and 2004 was $760,998 and $1,000,000, respectively. &nbsp;The interest rate was 8.25% and 6.25% at December 31, 2005 and 2004, respectively. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>On December 16, 2004, the Company closed on a $2,500,000 short term loan with Two River, secured by the property in Richmond, Virginia. The interest rate is 6.00%. &nbsp;&nbsp;The loan matured on September 16, 2005, however the Company prepaid this loan in April 2005. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company purchases securities on margin from time to time. &nbsp;At December 31, 2005 and 2004, the margin loan was $-0- and $6,010,154, respectively. &nbsp;The interest rate ranged from 4% to 6% during the year ended December 31, 2005 and from 3.0% to 4.0% during the year ended December 31, 2004. &nbsp;These loans are secured by investment securities with a market value of $8,107,492 and $12,130,692 at December 31, 2005 and 2004, respectively. These margin loans are due on demand.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>

<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt"><U>NOTE 9 - EMPLOYEE STOCK OPTION PLAN</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:72pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company&#146;s 1994 Stock Option Plan (the 1994 plan) expired on December 31, 2003. &nbsp;On September 28, 2004, the Company&#146;s shareholders approved and ratified the 2004 Stock Option Plan (the 2004 plan) authorizing the grant to officers and key employees of options to purchase up to 750,000 shares of common stock. &nbsp;Options may be granted at any time through September 27, 2014. &nbsp;No option shall be available for exercise beyond ten years. &nbsp;All options are exercisable after one year from date of grant. &nbsp;The option price shall not be below the fair market value at date of grant. &nbsp;Cancelled or expired options are added back to the pool of shares available under the 2004 plan. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company adopted the fair value recognition provisions of SFAS No. 123, &#147;Accounting for Stock Based Compensation&#148; on January 1, 2003. &nbsp;During the year ended December 31, 2005, 11 employees were granted options to purchase a total of 82,000 shares. &nbsp;The fair value of those options was approximately $0.65 based on assumptions noted below and the fair value is being amortized over the 1-year vesting period.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>

<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>NOTE 10 - DIVIDEND REINVESTMENT AND STOCK PURCHASE PLAN</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Effective August 28, 1995, the Company implemented a Dividend Reinvestment and Stock Purchase Plan (DRIP). &nbsp;Under the terms of the DRIP, shareholders who participate may reinvest all or part of their dividends in additional shares of the Company at approximately 95% of the market price. &nbsp;Shareholders may also purchase additional shares at approximately 95% of its market price by making optional cash payments. &nbsp;For the year ended December 31, 2005, 2004 and 2003, the Company received $4,948,282, $4,168,273 and $2,872,459, respectively. &nbsp;There were 861,670, 669,018 and 733,926, new shares issued, respectively.</P>

<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>NOTE 12 - INCOME TAXES</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company has elected to be taxed as a REIT. &nbsp;&nbsp;As the Company has not distributed all of its income currently, a provision for Federal excise and other taxes of $47,892, $121,646 and $107,370 has been made for the years ended December 31, 2005, 2004 and 2003, respectively. &nbsp;Included in the 2005 and 2004 tax provision is a Federal liability for built-in gains on the sale of assets acquired by the Company before its REIT election. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>NOTE 13 - PAYMENTS TO AFFILIATED PERSONS AND RELATED PARTY</U></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<U>TRANSACTIONS</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>Management currently operates two additional REITs, Monmouth Real Estate Investment Corporation and United Mobile Homes, Inc. &nbsp;Certain general and administrative expenses are shared among these companies based on use or service provided. &nbsp;Allocations of salaries and benefits are made based on the amount of employees and time dedicated to each affiliated Company. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Payments to Affiliated Persons</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>During 2005, 2004 and 2003, Eugene W. Landy, President of the Company, received $70,700, $53,200 and $70,700, respectively in salary, legal and director fees for these periods, respectively. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-64-</P>

<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Transactions with United Mobile Homes, Inc. (United)</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>United owns and operates manufactured home communities. &nbsp;Four Directors of the Company are also Directors and shareholders of United. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>During the years ended December 31, 2005, 2004, and 2003, the Company (MHS prior to December 6, 2001) sold 4, 3 and 4 manufactured homes, respectively, to United for total sales prices of $79,305, $64,824 and $78,195, respectively, at the Company&#146;s cost. &nbsp;These sales represented 100%, 39% and 29%, respectively, of the total sales made by the Company. &nbsp;These manufactured homes were available through the Company, but could have been acquired by United from a third party at approximately the same price. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>United financed/refinanced certain loans made by the Company to third parties during 2005 and 2004. &nbsp;The total amount financed was $10,500 and $70,150, respectively.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company from time to time holds common stock of United in its securities portfolio. &nbsp;During 2005, the Company purchased 50,000 shares of United in the open market. &nbsp;During 2004, the Company sold 11,000 shares of the common stock of United in the open market and recognized a gain of $103,705. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>United held the following amounts of the Convertible Subordinated Debentures as of December 31, 2005 and 2004:</P>

<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>Transactions with Monmouth Real Estate Investment Corporation (MREIC)</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>MREIC owns industrial properties on long-term net leases to credit tenants. &nbsp;Three Directors of the Company are also Directors of MREIC. &nbsp;The Company held 1,000 shares of common stock of MREIC in its securities portfolio at December 31, 2003. &nbsp;During 2004, the Company sold the common stock of MREIC in the open market and recognized a gain of $1,626.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>MREIC owned $500,000 of the 2013 Debentures as of December 31, 2005 and 2004.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>A Director of MREIC owns $250,000 of the 2013 Debenture and $100,000 of the 2015 Debenture as of December 31, 2005. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR>
<BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; padding-left:18pt; text-indent:-18pt; font-size:12pt" align=center>-65-</P>
<HR style="padding-top:7.2pt; padding-bottom:7.2pt" noshade size=1.333>
<P style="margin:0pt; page-break-before:always"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify><U>NOTE 14 &#150; GROUP CONCENTRATIONS OF CREDIT RISK</U></P>
<P style="margin:0pt"><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company owns 11 properties totaling 779,498 square feet of which 6 properties or 382,853 square feet (or 49%) are leased to Federal Express Corporation and subsidiaries (FDX). &nbsp;Rental income and reimbursements from FDX totaled approximately $2,281,000, $2,180,000 and $1,297,000, or 47%, 77% and 75%, for the years ended December 31, 2005, 2004, and 2003, respectively.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="line-height:14pt; margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-size:12pt" align=justify>The Company&#146;s loan portfolio is diversified. Generally, loans are collateralized by the manufactured homes. &nbsp;At December 31, 2005, 2004 and 2003, all loans were secured.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt" align=justify><BR></P>
